# MEP PDF Sizing Tool — Build Plan (authoritative spec)

> **Status:** living document. This is the source of truth. If a decision
> changes, update this file *in the same commit* as the code change.
>
> **Provenance note:** This plan was reconstructed from the project kickoff
> brief (the original 6 hand-off files were not available, so the build plan and
> the drafted engine/standards/test files were authored from scratch — see
> [§15 Decisions log](#15-decisions-log)). SNI values are draft placeholders
> tagged `// VERIFY` until transcribed from official SNI PDFs.

---

## 1. Overview & goal

An **offline, native Windows desktop application** for MEP (mechanical /
electrical / plumbing) design. An engineer:

1. loads **PDF floor plans**,
2. **calibrates each sheet's scale** and sets **per-floor heights**,
3. **drags duct / pipe elements** onto the plans,

and the app then automatically:

- **sizes** ducts, pipes, pumps and fire systems to **Indonesian SNI** standards,
- **solves the network** (node pressures / flows),
- **auto-draws schematic riser diagrams**,
- shows a **live pressure heatmap**, and
- outputs a **Bill of Materials (BOM)**,

all with **no internet connection**.

## 2. Product scope

Services covered, in MVP build order (see [§13](#13-open-decisions--chosen-defaults)):
ducts (HVAC) · clean/cold water · wastewater + vent → then rainwater, recycle,
pumps → then fire protection (sprinkler / hydrant / fire pump).

## 3. Stack — decided, not re-litigated

| Concern | Choice |
|---|---|
| Framework / target | **Flutter, Windows desktop**, compiled native (develop on any OS, ship Windows) |
| Calc engine | **Pure-Dart**, **zero Flutter imports**, in package `mechx_engine` |
| PDF rendering | `pdfrx` (PDFium) |
| Persistence | `drift` (SQLite) + versioned project files |
| State | Riverpod with undo/redo |
| Tests | `package:test` (engine), `flutter_test` (UI) |

**Explicitly NOT used:** web stack, Electron, WinUI, default Material/Fluent
theme, cloud, login, telemetry, browser storage.

## 4. Polish bar (acceptance criteria — not aspiration)

A screen is not "done" until it meets all of these:

- **Instant launch + viewport restore** — no spinner.
- **60/120 fps pan/zoom**, zero dropped frames on drag.
- **Direct manipulation** with snap feedback.
- **Native input** — scroll/pinch zoom, middle-drag pan, full keyboard shortcuts.
- **Async calc off the UI thread** — *no* modal "calculating…".
- **8pt grid, one type scale, light/dark.**
- **Motion that orients** — nothing decorative.

## 5. Module map / architecture

```
MechX/                              (Flutter app, package: mechx)
├─ lib/
│  ├─ main.dart                     app entry (P0 shell)
│  ├─ ui/                           screens, canvas, design system   (P0+)
│  ├─ data/                         drift schema, project file IO     (P0+)
│  └─ store/                        Riverpod providers, undo/redo      (P0+)
├─ test/                            widget / integration tests
├─ packages/
│  └─ mechx_engine/                 PURE-DART engine (no Flutter)
│     ├─ lib/
│     │  ├─ units.dart              SI typed quantities
│     │  ├─ hydraulics.dart         hydraulic-formula kernel
│     │  ├─ pressure_field.dart     heatmap scalar-field kernel
│     │  ├─ sizing/                 SNI sizing paths (ducts/water/...)  (P3+)
│     │  └─ standards/
│     │     └─ sni.dart             pluggable SNI standards data
│     └─ test/
│        ├─ hydraulics_test.dart    seed correctness anchors
│        └─ pressure_field_test.dart
└─ MEP-PDF-Sizing-Tool-Build-Plan.md (this file)
```

**Why a separate engine package** (deviation from a literal `lib/engine/`): the
guardrails demand a *pure-Dart* engine that runs under `dart test` with
`package:test` and >90% coverage. `dart test` cannot run inside a Flutter
package (the Flutter SDK dependency breaks pure-Dart resolution), so the engine
lives in its own pure package the app consumes by path. Fully reversible; see
[§15](#15-decisions-log).

## 6. Domain model & units

- **One internal unit system: SI.** Convert only at the UI boundary.
- **Typed quantities, never bare numbers across modules** (`units.dart`):
  `Length`, `Diameter`, `Area`, `Velocity`, `FlowRate`, `Pressure`, `Head`,
  `Power`, `Roughness` — zero-cost Dart 3 `extension type`s over `double`.
- Construct from engineering units via static helpers (`Diameter.mm`,
  `FlowRate.litersPerSecond`, `Pressure.kiloPascals`…); read via `inXxx` getters.

## 7. The three sizing code paths (kept separate — §12)

A shared "size a pipe" function silently breaks drainage and fire. The engine
exposes the *primitives* separately and the sizing layer composes the correct
path per service:

| Path | Method | Kernel entry points |
|---|---|---|
| **Pressurized** (supply, fire feed) | demand → velocity → friction | `reynolds`, `frictionFactor*`, `headLossDarcy`, `headLossHazenWilliams` |
| **Gravity** (drainage) | Manning partial-full + stack capacity | `manningVelocity`, `manningFlowFull` |
| **Fire** | density/area + standpipe residual | (P5) builds on pressurized + dedicated residual checks |

**Supply pump-head sizing** (`sizing/supply_design.dart`, pressurized path):
`H_pump = H_static + H_friction + H_residual`, where `H_static` is the
floor-elevation rise to the critical fixture (§10), `H_friction` the critical-path
loss, and `H_residual` the **design target residual pressure** at the fixture.
That target is an **engineer-set design choice (NOT an SNI value)**: recommended
**2.25 bar** within a **2.0–2.5 bar** comfort band — deliberately above the SNI
minimums (~0.49–0.98 bar) and below the practical max (~3.92 bar) / mandatory
pressure-relief threshold (4.9 bar), so fixtures perform well without forcing a
PRV/zone on lower floors.

## 8. Standards as pluggable data + the VERIFY list

- `standards/sni.dart` implements `StandardsProfile`; the engine depends on the
  **interface**, never the concrete numbers — standards are swappable data.
- Every value is wrapped in `StandardValue<T>` carrying
  `{value, unit, citation, verified, sourceUrl, note}`. `verified == true` means
  the figure was found in the **SNI text itself** and corroborated; `false`
  means a placeholder OR a real figure sourced only from **secondary literature**
  (clause not yet confirmed against the official PDF). The UI/output must show
  every `verified == false` value as **UNVERIFIED**. `SniProfile.verifyChecklist`
  returns the outstanding items, most-critical first.

**Seeding status (SNI 8153:2015, researched 2026-06 from the archive.org full
text + Indonesian engineering literature):**

| Value | Figure | `verified` | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Min pressure at fixture outlet | 0.50 kgf/cm² (49.03 kPa) | ✅ | verbatim SNI text |
| Min pressure at flush valve | 1 kgf/cm² (98.07 kPa) | ✅ | verbatim SNI text |
| Mandatory pressure-relief threshold | >5 kgf/cm² (490.33 kPa) | ✅ | verbatim SNI text |
| Demand method (UBAP + Hunter curve) | — | ✅ | confirmed prescribed method |
| Max fixture pressure (zoning target) | ~4 kgf/cm² (392.27 kPa) | ⚠️ | secondary design guidance |
| Max supply velocity | 2.0 m/s | ⚠️ | secondary consensus |
| Demand-curve point **values** (Gambar 1) | two branches | ⚠️ | chart read-offs (UPC-2012 lineage) |
| UBAP fixture-unit table (Tabel 3) | per fixture/occupancy | ⚠️ | secondary sources |
| Drain velocity cap | 3.0 m/s | ⚠️ | not an SNI clause (slope+UBAP based) |

> The two ⚠️ items called out in [§13.4](#13-open-decisions--chosen-defaults)
> (max fixture pressure, demand curve) are now seeded with **real, sourced**
> figures but remain flagged until confirmed against the official SNI PDF.
> NB: **SNI 8153:2025** now supersedes the 2015 edition.

## 9. One solve feeds everything (no parallel calculations — §12)

The **schematic diagram** and the **pressure heatmap** are *generated renders of
the solved network*, never independent calculations that could disagree with the
sizing numbers. A single node-pressure/flow solve feeds: sizing, pump duty, the
auto diagram, and the heatmap. `pressure_field.dart` only *interpolates* already
solved node values into a grid — it computes no physics.

## 10. Geometry & length — single source of truth

- **Horizontal length = pixels × calibrated scale** (per sheet).
- **Vertical (riser) length = floor-elevation delta** (per-floor heights).
- **Never** measure riser length from a PDF. Geometry + floor elevations are the
  authoritative source for every length the engine consumes.

## 11. Persistence & versioning

- **Versioned project file format + DB schema from day one** (`drift`/SQLite).
- Project files carry a schema/version header; migrations are explicit.
- Viewport / last-open state persisted for instant restore (§4).

## 12. Architecture guardrails (non-negotiable)

1. Engine is **pure** (no Flutter imports), **>90% test coverage**, seeded with
   SNI/textbook worked examples.
2. **One unit system internally (SI)**; convert only at the UI boundary; typed
   quantities, never bare numbers between modules.
3. **Geometry + floor elevations are the single source of truth for length**
   ([§10](#10-geometry--length--single-source-of-truth)).
4. **Gravity, pressurized, and fire are SEPARATE sizing code paths**
   ([§7](#7-the-three-sizing-code-paths-kept-separate--12)).
5. **Diagram + heatmap are generated renders of the one solve**
   ([§9](#9-one-solve-feeds-everything-no-parallel-calculations--12)).
6. **Standards are pluggable data**; `// VERIFY` values are flagged as
   unverified in UI/output ([§8](#8-standards-as-pluggable-data--the-verify-list)).
7. **Versioned project file format + DB schema from day one.**

## 13. Open decisions & chosen defaults

1. **MVP service order:** ducts + clean water + wastewater (+vent) first;
   rainwater/recycle/pumps in P4; fire in P5.
2. **Source architecture template:** ground water tank → transfer pump → roof
   tank → gravity downfeed + top-zone booster.
3. **Heatmap default:** residual-pressure metric, per-zone colour scaling;
   fill-ratio (not pressure) for gravity drainage.
4. **VERIFY priority:** pull SNI 8153 **max-fixture-pressure** (zoning trigger)
   and the **demand-curve** table to the top of the verify list — flag to the
   user, do not guess.

## 14. Phase plan

| Phase | Scope | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| **Step 1** ✅ | Scaffold + pure engine + seed tests green | *all seed tests pass* |
| **P0 Shell** 🟡 | Custom design system, multi-sheet nav, pannable/zoomable canvas (✅ done & tested); **pdfrx PDF render** (⬜ next, behind the `sheetContentBuilderProvider` seam) | review stop |
| **P1** ✅ | Project details + per-floor heights + scale calibration. Pure geometry (calibration + elevations), editable project/floor state + inspector UI, pdfrx PDF import + render (2.4.4, "Open PDF…" → sheet-per-page), and the on-canvas mark-a-known-distance calibrate tool. All logic tested (95 total). | — |
| **P2** ✅ | Drawing (incl. risers). Pure network model (nodes/edges/services/lengths), drawing controller (run polylines w/ snapping, risers, undo/redo/clear), canvas network render + rubber-band overlay, and the inspector draw palette (tool/service/undo). 109 tests. | — |
| **P3** ✅ | SNI sizing engine. Three independent §7 paths (duct/water/drainage, parallel agents), a network dispatcher with **branching flow accumulation** + `autoSizeNetwork`, **grille/diffuser face-velocity sizing** (noise-driven), and a live on-canvas sizing display (DN/Ø labels) with an inspector toggle. Engine 145 tests, app 45. | — |
| **P4** ✅ | Node-pressure solver (the §12 keystone), pump duty, downfeed zoning, BOM aggregator, pressure heatmap (generated render of the solve), auto schematic riser diagram. Live inspector results. | **MVP = P0–P4 ✅** |
| **P5** ✅ | Fire protection — sprinkler density/area sizing, standpipe/hydrant flow + fire-pump duty; inspector fire panel. SNI 03-3989-2000 (sprinkler) + SNI 03-1745-2000 (standpipe) values seeded from web research; `// VERIFY` retained on engineering assumptions only. | — |

Commit per logical unit; keep tests passing continuously. **Pause at each phase
boundary for review.**

| 2026-07-30 | **iSystem — the phase-imbalance warning is raised only when the imbalance is actually REDUCIBLE (user-reported)** | A user's three-phase board (two 3φ sub-panel feeders + two EQUAL 1φ lighting ways) reported `Phase loading is unbalanced by 22.3% … Redistribute single-phase circuits` — an instruction that cannot be carried out: two equal single-phase ways can never load three lines evenly, and `balancePhases` had already placed them as evenly as they go. The engine, not the engineer, assigns phases, so "redistribute" is only a real instruction when SOMETHING is holding the board off a better assignment (a phase PIN, or a prior-assignment seed the stability threshold refused to leave). Added a judge-only actionability gate in `electrical/compute.dart`: pure, unit-tested `minimumPhaseSpreadA(currents)` returns the smallest line-current spread ANY assignment of a board's single-phase way currents could reach — three-phase ways are excluded (they load all three lines equally, so they shift every sum alike and cannot change the spread) and phase PINS are RELEASED (unpinning is an action the engineer can take, so a pin-forced imbalance counts as reducible). The search is EXHAUSTIVE hence exact while the way count is ≤ `kPhaseExactSearchMaxWays` (10), with the heaviest way fixed on the first line for symmetry (3^(n-1) walks); above that the balancer's own greedy + relocate/swap refinement, re-run free of pins and prior seeding, is the reference. Because the average line current is assignment-invariant, the achievable IMBALANCE is that floor measured against the same average: the warning now fires only when it is over `kPhaseImbalanceWarnPercent` (15, the pre-existing literal, now named) AND a re-assignment would save more than `kPhaseImbalanceReducibleMarginPercent` (1.0 pp) — and the message names the real action (`Unpin the 3 ways pinned to a phase …` when pins are the cause) plus the figure that is achievable, instead of a generic "redistribute". The floor search runs ONLY for a board already over the limit, so an even board costs nothing. The percentage itself is untouched and still prints on the schedule TOTAL footer, the panel inspector and both reports — only the unactionable WARNING is dropped, and with it (by construction, since it reads the warning) the canvas card's `imbalance NN%` badge. Additive `PhaseBalanceResult.imbalanceReducible` (default false) carries the verdict, and the `feeder-below-fed-demand` message now offers "rebalance that board's single-phase ways" only when it is true, else names the imbalance as inherent and leaves the feeder rating as the fix. NO sizing change anywhere (the balance, incomer, busbar and demand current are byte-identical — this is a reporting gate). Tests: new `electrical_phase_imbalance_actionable_test.dart` (16 cases — the floor function hand-enumerated incl. the indivisible 3+3+2+2+2 → 2 case and the >10-way fallback; the reported board reproduced to R 27.9 / S 27.9 / T 22.1 A / 22.3 % and proven unwarned; pin-forced imbalance warned + worded + cleared by unpinning; the feeder cause text). Re-baselined: `electrical_compute_test`'s balance case (its 14.4 A + 8.7 A pair is likewise irreducible ⇒ now asserts NO warning) and the two report characterization pins (both fixture boards are unbalanceable ⇒ −5 lines). Gate: engine **1487** / app **1230** / analyze clean; goldens byte-identical (the sample project's boards never carried the warning). |

| 2026-07-27 | **iSystem — honest LV service: no fabricated TRAFO/MVMDP/LVMDP on the source spine (user-reported) + a `feeder-below-fed-demand` check found by a whole-building simulation** | User-reported with a canvas screenshot (`PLN 33000 kVA -> TRAFO 10 kVA -> LVMDP` drawn above a plain low-voltage sub-panel): *"why is it still showing trafo and lvmdp when it's not a medium voltage system"*, then *"try drawing a whole house/building system and see the result, debug what is wrong"*. **Root cause:** both spine builders in `report/electrical_sld_drawing.dart` — `_buildSourceSpine` (vertical: overview/riser/exports) and `_buildSourceSpineH` (horizontal: the interactive single-line canvas) — hard-coded the TRANSFORMER + LV-main chain (only the MV bus was gated, and on `sources != null || dualTransformer || transformerKva != null`), and `buildElectricalSourceSpine` draws for ANY project with demand — so a plain LV (TR) direct PLN service got a FABRICATED customer substation, with the trafo kVA auto-derived from demand via the genset ladder (the '10 kVA' the user saw), and even a genset alone wrongly implied an MV station. **Fix:** new `_isMvService` (= explicit `transformerKva` OR `dualTransformer` — a DECLARED MV service only; distributed genset/solar/battery/capacitor never imply MV) gates the whole MVMDP -> TRANSFORMER -> LVMDP chain in BOTH builders. An LV service draws the honest SUPPLY HEAD alone — plain `PLN` (+ declared capacity; never 'MV STATION'), the head label moved above the symbol, side sources on a stacked drop off the head (the vertical builder gains the same stack idiom the horizontal one had; declared-MV keeps its historic coordinates byte-identical), the earthing designation at the SERVICE ENTRANCE, and a short outgoing lead into the root board. `buildElectricalSourceSpine`'s emptiness gate now also counts `supplyKind`/`supplyCapacityVa`. The Sources editor's connection-capacity field switched **kVA -> VA** (daya tersambung is quoted in VA — typing 33000 into the old kVA field had stored 33 MVA, the screenshot's 'PLN 33000 kVA'). **Whole-building sim:** a scratch widget-test harness (archived to the session scratchpad, not committed) drove the REAL store intents (`addPanelAt`/`addFedPanelAt`(+templates)/`addCircuit`/`setGenerator`/`setSupplyCapacityVa`/`setPanelLayoutPos`) to build a 1-phase 220 V HOUSE (Panel Rumah, 6 ways, PLN 5500 VA) and a 3-storey BUILDING (LVMDP + 3 floor LPs + PP Pompa + genset, boards placed per floor), and captured/inspected the single-line, board-schedule and building-riser states. Verified good: the honest head + earth + no substation on both fixtures; BRI-style schedules (RCD tokens, WATT DAYA, TOTAL + imbalance); riser floor bands with FFL + fan-outs. **Found + fixed — `feeder-below-fed-demand`:** a feeder way sizes on the fed board's diversified demand W at a balanced pf (`_effectiveLoadW` <- `panelDemandW`), while the fed board's own incomer sizes on its WORST-PHASE line current (`demandCurrentA = max(l1,l2,l3)`) — on an imbalanced fed board the feeder breaker can print BELOW what the board draws (sim: PP Pompa 27.1 A worst phase at 24.3% imbalance vs its 25 A feeder — the issued schedule contradicted itself two columns apart). Added a JUDGE-ONLY `computeSystem` post-pass warning (never resizes — the honest fix is rebalancing the fed board's ways or a breaker override; locatable `panelId`+`circuitId`, fans into Review via the generic `electrical:<code>` kind with zero app wiring). Engine tests derive the premise from the engine's own results (imbalanced fed board warns; balanced doesn't); the two report characterization pins re-baselined (+2 warning lines, new FNV hashes). **Recorded, not fixed:** riser feeder-branch labels can overlap a sibling board's box when several boards share a floor band (wants the `placeEdgeLabel`-style collision pass the mech riser has); device-kA tokens read 25kA on a domestic board (the N9 fallback tier from the 16 kA project default — an honest service-size-derived fault level is future work). Spine tests rewritten to the new semantics (LV head-only incl. horizontal earth; capacitor/genset no longer imply MV; declared-MV chain + MVMDP unchanged; dual-tx auto-sizes the trafo kVA). Gate: engine **1456**, app **1218**, `flutter analyze` clean; goldens 05/08/11 re-captured (the sample project is an LV service => head-only spine) + visually verified. |

| 2026-07-26 | **iSystem — the standard desktop accelerators (Ctrl+N/I/P, Ctrl+1..8, Ctrl+, , Ctrl+Shift+P) + an F1 keyboard-shortcuts sheet (user-reported)** | The shell answered to only FOUR global combos (Ctrl/Cmd+K / S / Shift+S / O), so the commands an engineer's hands already know — New, Import, Print, Undo off-canvas, jump-to-a-destination — had no key. Added a pure, unit-tested accelerator TABLE (`ui/shell/shell_shortcuts.dart`): `matchShellShortcut(key, {mod, shift, alt})` → a `ShellCommand`, with the shell (`app_shell.dart`) reduced to a dispatch `switch` over it that runs the SAME entry points the top bar / nav rail / palette use (so an accelerator can never diverge from its clicked equivalent). New bindings: **Ctrl+N** new project · **Ctrl+I** import plan · **Ctrl+P** export the annotated plan PDF (the app's "print", `runExportGuarded` as usual) · **Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y / Ctrl+Shift+Z** undo/redo now GLOBAL (the one `historyProvider` timeline the canvases already drive, so undo works on the hub screens where nothing holds focus — the I1 dead spot) · **Ctrl+Shift+P** as a palette alias beside Ctrl+K · **Ctrl+1..8** (and their numpad twins) for the nav-rail destinations in rail order (Layout · Riser · Electrical · Building · Review · Commercial · Projects · Preferences) · **Ctrl+,** for Preferences · **F1** for the shortcuts sheet. Two safety rules are part of the table: **Alt never matches** (Windows AltGr reports as Ctrl+Alt, so swallowing it would eat real characters on an ID/EU layout) and `ShellCommand.deferToTextField` marks undo/redo as combos a focused field owns (the shell returns false and the framework's `DefaultTextEditingShortcuts` edit the FIELD, never the drawing). Deliberately NOT claimed globally: the canvas zoom family (Ctrl +/-/0), the selection combos (Ctrl+A/C/V, Delete) and Esc, which stay with the focused canvas; and Ctrl+Q, which is not a Windows quit convention and would only add an accidental-exit path (Alt+F4 / the close button already route through the `_ExitGuard`). Discoverability: a new `ui/shell/shortcuts_sheet.dart` `ShortcutsSheetOverlay` — an F1 cheat sheet (gated on `shortcutsSheetOpenProvider`, renders NOTHING when closed) listing the global bindings beside the CANVAS ones (tools V/R/E/M/K/B, ortho O, service 1-9, Shift+R/M, hold-Space pan, F fit, Ctrl+0 …) so the whole keyboard is learnable in one place; plus keycaps on the matching command-palette rows and a 'Keyboard shortcuts' palette command. Esc closes the sheet pre-focus (it carries no text field, so the bubble listener would never see the key on a hub screen), and opening either overlay closes the other so two scrims can't stack. Tests: `shell_shortcuts_test` (9 cases — the bindings, the Alt rule, the deliberately-unclaimed keys, and a pin that every documented keycap really resolves to the command it advertises AND that no global command is undocumented) + 5 new `shell_hotkeys_test` widget cases (Ctrl+N from a hub, Ctrl+1..8/Ctrl+, navigation, F1/Esc, the two overlays never stacking, and Ctrl+Z standing down inside a focused text field). UI-only; no engine / `.mechx` / version change; goldens byte-identical (both overlays are closed at rest). Gate: analyze clean, engine **1377**, app **1125**. |

| 2026-07-01 | **iSystem — bundle the ODA File Converter at release-build time (activate DWG import out-of-the-box)** | The DWG-import bundling hook (`installer/iSystem.iss` → `{app}\oda\`) existed but the binary was never in the build, so shipped installers reported "converter not found". CI/installer/docs only — no Dart change; app **478** / engine **1057** unaffected; release YAML validated. Activating it needs the maintainer's one-time bundle upload, then the NEXT release ships DWG support automatically. |
| 2026-06-30 | **iSystem — portable `.mechx`: embed the source plans in the project file** | Previously a `.mechx` stored only the FILE PATH to each PDF/DXF/DWG plan, so moving a project to another machine broke every sheet's background. Now **Save embeds the source plans** into the file so it is self-contained. Pure model: `ProjectDocument.assets` (`Map<String,String>` = source path → base64 bytes) + a `withSheets(...)` copy helper; serialized only when non-empty ⇒ a path-only project stays BYTE-IDENTICAL, tolerant/absent on load, no version bump (still v2). App layer `lib/data/project_assets.dart`: `gatherSheetAssets` reads each DISTINCT sheet source (the rendered file — `pdfPath`, else `dxfPath`; a multi-page PDF's shared path is embedded ONCE; a DWG sheet embeds its CONVERTED DXF so the receiver needs no ODA converter) → base64, skipping missing/unreadable files; `rehydrateAssets` extracts each embedded asset to a stable local file under `mechx_assets/` (systemTemp, content-hash name) and repoints the sheet paths, so the existing path-based PDF/DXF renderers work unchanged. `_saveProject` writes the EMBEDDED doc to disk but keeps the path-only encoding as the autosave "clean baseline" signature (so a just-saved project leaves no phantom recovery, and recovery snapshots stay lightweight — autosave never embeds); `_openProject` (and recovery-restore, a no-op there) rehydrate before `applyDocument`. Tests: `project_assets_test` (5: multi-page dedup, missing-file skip, embed→encode→decode→rehydrate recovers original bytes after the source is deleted, DWG-embeds-its-DXF, no-asset byte-identical), `project_document_test` already covers the `assets`-absent path. No engine change; goldens untouched (Save/Open only). Gate: app **476**, `flutter analyze` clean. Note: the ORIGINAL DWG isn't embedded (its converted DXF is) — reopening a DWG-sourced project on a converter-less machine works; re-importing the raw DWG still needs ODA. **Follow-up (same day): the embedded plans are gzip-compressed** before base64 (`gatherSheetAssets` = `base64(gzip(bytes))`), clawing back base64's ~33 % inflation — on the real 19 MB `EL10011003.dxf` the embedded payload drops from ~25 MB (raw base64) to ~3.1 MB, an **88 %** reduction. `rehydrateAssets` detects gzip by the `1f 8b` magic and gunzips, passing a raw-base64 payload through unchanged, so the uncompressed portable files written earlier the same day still open (backward-compatible). Tests extended (`project_assets_test`: gzip magic + decompress, compressed < raw-base64, legacy raw-base64 passthrough). Gate: app **478**, analyze clean. |

The Windows installer (`installer/iSystem.iss`) bundles `installer/vendor/oda/*` → `{app}\oda\` with `skipifsourcedoesntexist` (the ODA binary is NOT redistributed in-repo — `installer/vendor/oda/README.md` tells the maintainer to drop it in; absent ⇒ build still succeeds, DWG import reports "converter not found", PDF/DXF unaffected). New pure-engine `geometry/dxf_drawing.dart` `parseDxf(String) -> DxfDrawing?` reads the `ENTITIES` section — `LINE`, `LWPOLYLINE`, old-style `POLYLINE`/`VERTEX`, `CIRCLE`, `ARC` — and expands `INSERT` block references from the `BLOCKS` section with a baked translate·rotate·scale affine (recursive, depth-capped at 8; primitive-count capped at 400k). It is CRLF-tolerant, resyncs past malformed pairs, skips unrecognised entities (TEXT/DIMENSION/HATCH/SPLINE/3D), and returns `null` for a geometry-less file — never throws. The top-bar import button (`_pickAndLoadPlan`) now accepts **pdf + dxf** and branches by extension (single-sheet DXF skips the page picker); relabelled **"Import PDF" → "Import plan"** (`Impor denah`). Verified the parser against the client's real 19 MB `EL10011003.dxf` (2315 polylines + 409 circles + 145 arcs) + the 10 MB `EL1004 Diagram Panel BRI.dxf` (19724 polylines) — rendered to an SVG→Chromium PNG: boards, busbars, feeders, schedule grid + panel-detail blocks all correct, normalised, Y-flipped. Wired into the app Export menu as **'Building single-line (overview)' → PDF / DXF** (`electrical_export.dart` `exportElectricalOverviewPdf`/`Dxf`; `electrical_view.dart` `_ExportMenu` two new rows; 3 new localized `StringKey`s EN+ID). Visually verified by building a DXF-shaped 17-panel hierarchy (LVMDP → 4 MDP → PP boards, emergency branch) and rasterising via engine→SVG→Chromium: clean tiered tree, emergency sub-tree fully red, compact nodes, no overlap. New `electrical_sld_drawing_test` overview group (6 cases: one node per panel, essential propagation, depth tiering, legend, PDF red stroke, DXF ACI-red). | 2026-06-23 | **Loop split done properly — real geometry + a stability finding** | Threaded `building` + `calibrations` into `autoSizeNetwork` so a looped ring's Hardy-Cross split uses **real edge lengths** — runs via calibration, **risers via the §10 elevation delta** instead of a nominal connector (pixel-length fallback kept for the geometry-less path); wired through `sizingProvider`. While implementing the recommended sizing↔diameter fixed-point iteration I found it is **unstable**: sizing a leg to a target velocity gives D ∝ √Q, so Hazen–Williams resistance k ∝ L·Q^−2.435 and head loss h_f ∝ L·Q^−0.583 *decreases* with flow — an unstable equilibrium that drives the longer leg's flow toward zero. So the iteration was dropped in favour of the stable, standard length/resistance split at a consistent ring diameter (residual loop-vs-tree friction from per-segment diameters is second-order, `// VERIFY` for strongly non-uniform rings). New engine test proves a riser leg balances on its elevation-delta length (split differs with vs without geometry). Engine 353 + app 88 tests green, analyze clean. |

| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — inline valve/fitting minor losses folded into the solve** | The deferred `// VERIFY` from the component work: valves/strainers/meters now contribute **local head loss h = K·v²/2g** to the ONE pressurized solve. New `hydraulics.minorLossHead(k, velocity)` + a `NodeComponentInfo.minorLossK` table (gate 0.2, check 2.0, balancing 3.0, strainer 2.0, water meter 6.0; everything else 0 — pumps add head, tanks are sources, a PRV stays 0 because the PRV **zoning** sets its downstream pressure, not a K). Both `solvePressurized` and `solveDownfeed` add the minor loss when the BFS ARRIVES at a restrictor node (velocity from the feeding edge), so it propagates to every downstream node — exactly where a real valve throttles. K-values are representative fully-open general-practice numbers (Crane TP-410-class), **NOT an SNI clause** (flagged `// VERIFY` against the project's valve schedule). Strictly additive: a node with no component (or a non-restrictor) has K = 0 ⇒ **byte-identical** to before, so the sample project + all goldens are unchanged. Gate: engine **706** (+2 solve tests: a gate valve lifts required pump head by exactly K·v²/2g; a K=0 component at constant role changes nothing), app **258**, analyze clean. |

New `duct_sizing.airVelocityPressurePa` + `ductFittingLossPa(c, flow, diameter)` and a `NodeComponentInfo.airLossC` table (VCD 0.2, fire 0.3, motorized 0.4, VAV box 1.5 — representative HVAC practice, NOT an SNI clause, `// VERIFY`). The BFS adds the loss when it ARRIVES at a restrictor (velocity from the feeding duct), propagating downstream — exactly mirroring the valve fold. Additive: C = 0 (no component / not a restrictor) ⇒ byte-identical, so the sample + all goldens are unchanged. Gate: engine **707** (+a damper-adds-C·ρv²/2 test), app **259**, analyze clean, goldens unchanged. |

| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — discipline layers split by SYSTEM (one "Plumbing" bucket → Water/Sanitary/Storm/Fire)** | "Lumping water + drainage + storm + fire all in Plumbing doesn't make sense." The `DisciplineLayer` enum is now **6 system layers** — `water` (cold+hot), `sanitary` (drainage+vent), `storm` (rainwater), `fire` (sprinkler+hydrant), `hvac` (air), `electrical` — replacing `{plumbing,hvac,electrical}`. `disciplineOf(service)` maps each `ServiceType` to its system; `servicesFor` is derived from it (so they never drift), `isMechanical` = everything but electrical, default active = `water`. The DRAW chips + node palette already scope to `servicesFor(active)`, so each layer now shows only its system's services; the equipment groups re-scoped to their system (Plant/Valves/Meters → Water, Drains → Sanitary+Storm, Fire protection → Fire, air groups → HVAC). The Layout top-bar layer switcher renders all 6 (the sheet breadcrumb capped at 200 px + the switcher horizontally scrollable so 6 fit without clipping). Pure app-side (no engine change). Gate: app **259** (layer_store/layout/electrical tests updated for the system layers), engine 707 (unchanged), analyze clean; goldens 01/02/06 regenerated for the new switcher, verified. |

| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — fix: middle-click pan on the Layout canvas** | Middle-drag pan didn't work because the Layout sheet stacks its interaction overlays (selection / drawing / measure / tank, all `HitTestBehavior.opaque`) ON TOP of the `CanvasView`, so they swallowed the middle-button drag before it reached `CanvasView`'s own pointer listener underneath. Moved middle-button pan to an **ancestor `Listener`** wrapping the whole sheet stack (in `_SharedSheet`) — pointer events dispatch to the full hit-test path, so the ancestor receives the middle-drag even when an opaque overlay is the target; it drives the live canvas via the per-sheet `canvasKeyFor(...)` + a new public `CanvasView.panByScreen(delta)`. The now-redundant (always-blocked) middle-pan code was removed from `CanvasView`. Wheel-zoom / left-drag-as-scale unchanged. Gate: app **259**, analyze clean, goldens unchanged. |
| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — connection-by-drag (both disciplines) + canvas-input regression fixes** | Completing the "drag to connect" pair the user asked for, plus the input bugs the opaque overlays caused. **Canvas-input fixes** (same root cause as the middle-pan fix — the `HitTestBehavior.opaque` overlays sit ON TOP of `CanvasView` and swallowed events): (1) **palette drag-drop placement** — the `DropOverlay` (`DragTarget`) was stacked BELOW the selection overlay so a dragged card was hit-tested by selection first and never reached the drop target; moved the `DropOverlay` ABOVE selection (it `IgnorePointer`s itself when idle so normal taps still fall through); (2) **mouse-wheel zoom** — handled on the same ancestor `Listener` that drives middle-pan via a new public `CanvasView.zoomByScroll`. **Mechanical fixture→main tap:** dragging a FREE node (a fixture/terminal just dropped from the palette, with no edges) and releasing it near a horizontal run now connects it — `endNodeDragWithSnap` routes a no-target release to `_tapFreeNodeIntoNearestEdge`, which finds the nearest run edge within snap radius, splits it at the projected nearest point (or reuses an endpoint if within snap²) and draws a NEW branch `NetEdge` from the fixture to that junction carrying the main's service; the fixture stays put, only a connecting pipe is added; one undo step. Connected nodes / resize-handle endpoints are unaffected (the tap only fires for an edgeless node). **Riser node:** a `NodeComponent.riser` marker (role `main`) in the palette NODES group with an up/down-arrow symbol — place it where a riser meets the main, then connect runs from it; drops/renders/persists like the other components. **Electrical load re-parent:** on the single-line canvas a load node is now a `Draggable<_LoadRef>` and each panel card a `DragTarget<_LoadRef>` → `ElectricalProjectController.moveCircuit(from, circuitId, to)` lifts the way to the dropped-on panel (field-preserving via `_withProject`; refuses same-panel, a feeder onto the panel it feeds, and unknown ids), so the load re-sizes under its new board. Additive/pure-UI + store; engine math untouched. Gate: engine **707** (+riser role), app **265** (+ fixture-tap, drag-drop e2e, 4 moveCircuit tests), analyze clean; goldens 01/02 regenerated for the Riser-node card, otherwise unchanged. |
| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — lay mains by pulling a line out of a node (replacing drag-on pipe segments)** | The user: "instead of making pipes as segments to drag onto the canvas lets use the riser to act like a starting point where we drag lines out of them to lay mainline pipes." Mirrors the electrical outlet-drag. New `NetworkController.drawRunFromNode(fromId, world, {service, snapRadius})`: pulls a NEW run out of an existing node — the far end SNAPS to a node within the radius, else TAPS into the nearest run (splitting it at the projected point via the shared `_resolveDrawEndpoint`), else drops a fresh junction; the run inherits the source node's existing service (else the active draw service); one undo step. UI: `NetworkSelectionOverlay` is now stateful and gives every **non-fixture** node (riser / fitting / plant — the mainline sources) a small accent **outlet nub** offset up-right of its move handle; dragging it rubber-bands a dashed preview line (`_PullPainter`) and lays the run on release (terminals stay branch-only via the existing fixture-tap drag). The palette's **pipe/duct segment cards are removed** — the Riser is now the headline "MAINLINE START" card with the new pull guidance; Fitting/Terminal + the equipment groups stay (`addSegment` + the drop-overlay segment path are retained for compatibility/tests but no card produces them). Pure app-side (store + overlay + palette); engine untouched. Gate: engine 707 (unchanged), app **270** (+5 drawRunFromNode tests; palette-render test updated), analyze clean; goldens 01/02 regenerated (outlet nubs + the new palette), visually verified. |
| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — pipes drawn as real pipes with fittings (coupling / elbow / tee / cross / cap)** | The user: "make the pipes not just a single line and show actual pipe and the connections (mug/elbow/tee etc)." `NetworkLayer`'s painter now draws each run as a **walled pipe body** — a darker casing stroke with the service-colour bore inside it (two visible wall lines), its on-screen width tiered by the sized nominal bore (`_pipeOuterPx`: DN15→5 px … DN150+→13 px; ducts fatter), kept constant at any zoom. While drawing the pipes the painter records each junction's incident pipe directions (a `_Joint`) and then draws the matching **fitting** in a steel colour over the joins: an open **END CAP** (1 pipe, a perpendicular bar), an in-line **COUPLING** sleeve (2 pipes ~collinear, dot < −0.94), an **ELBOW** corner body (2 pipes at an angle), or a **TEE / CROSS** hub (3 / 4+). Only plain junctions (role `main`, no equipment `component`) become a fitting — fixtures / plant / equipment keep their own glyph, and a fitting-bearing junction drops the old bare dot (a free main node with no pipe yet still shows it). Honours the layer fade/visibility (fittings fade with their coordination layer). Pure app-side render (one file, `network_layer.dart`); no engine / store / model change. Gate: engine 707 (unchanged), app **270** (unchanged — purely visual), analyze clean; goldens 01/02/03 regenerated (walled pipes + the tee hub at the branch), visually verified light + dark. |
| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — pipe width tracks DN, selectable junction fittings, stock-length coupling joints** | Three follow-ups to the pipe render. **(1) Width by DN, always:** the painter now reads `sizingProvider` for the pipe WIDTH unconditionally (the show-sizes toggle now only gates the size LABELS), and `_pipeOuterPx` is a CONTINUOUS map of the sized bore → screen px (clamped 4–16 px pipes, 8–20 px ducts) — so a pipe visibly grows with its DN even with labels off. **(2) Selectable fittings:** new pure-engine `JunctionFitting` enum (`auto/coupling/elbow/tee/wye/teeWye/cross/cap` + `.label`) and additive `NetNode.fittingType` (copyWith + clear flag; `.mechx` round-trip, tolerant; carried through every store NetNode-rebuild path) — renamed from `FittingType` to avoid colliding with the BOM enum. The fitting body is now built from fat metal **arms along the ACTUAL incident pipe directions** (so a tee reads as a T, an elbow as an L, a cross as +, a wye keeps its angled branch + a filled crotch gusset; a coupling is an inline sleeve, a cap a flat end face). `auto` derives the type from the joint's degree/collinearity; **right-click a junction → Fitting menu** (`showNodeFittingMenu`, reusing the edge-menu widgets) pins it via `setNodeFittingType`. **(3) Coupling joints every stock length:** when the sheet is calibrated, a run is marked with a coupling tick every stock pipe length — **4 m PVC/PPR**, **6 m steel (sprinkler/hydrant)** — computed from the sheet's m/px (`_stockLengthM`/`_couplingMark`); uncalibrated ⇒ no marks, ducts excluded. Store/engine sizing math untouched (a drawing/BOM concern). Gate: engine **707** (+`JunctionFitting` round-trip in the app `.mechx` test), app **271** (+setNodeFittingType test; .mechx fitting round-trip), analyze clean; goldens 01/02/03/06 regenerated (DN widths + arm fittings; 06 calibrated shows the coupling joints), visually verified (incl. a calibrated preview confirming evenly-spaced 4 m couplings + the tee/wye/caps). |

| 2026-06-25 | **iSystem — pipe cut-plan efficiency engine (couplings minimise stock wastage)** | "There should be an efficiency engine on where the couplings are to minimise material/pipe wastage." New pure-engine `sizing/pipe_optimizer.dart`: **(a)** `buildPipeChains` merges maximal **collinear** same-(service,diameter) run segments through degree-2 pass-through vertices into ONE continuous pipe (breaking at branches/bends/reducers/risers; lengths via `edgeLength`), returning per-edge chain arc-length offsets; **(b)** `planStockCuts` is a first-fit-decreasing **cutting-stock** packer — a run > one stock bar uses whole bars + a remainder, and remainders are packed to **reuse offcuts**, minimising bars purchased (`StockCutPlan` reports bars + `wastePercent`); **(c)** `buildPipeCutPlan` groups chains by (service, diameter) at 4 m PVC/PPR / 6 m steel. **Render:** couplings now fall at stock boundaries along the WHOLE chain (not reset per segment, so only the chain tail is a cut piece), and a collinear pass-through vertex draws **no** fitting glyph (the stock couplings are the joints) — driven by `pipeChainsProvider` → per-edge `_EdgeCut`. **UI:** the Review hub gains a **Pipe cut plan** card + an efficiency stat row (stock pipes · pipe required · offcut waste %). `pipeChainsProvider`/`pipeCutPlanProvider` in `solve_store`. Pure-additive; no sizing/hydraulic change. Gate: engine **717** (+10 optimizer/chain tests), app **272** (+`pipeCutPlanProvider` seam test), analyze clean; goldens unchanged (demo has no collinear chains), visually verified on a calibrated collinear preview (continuous 4 m couplings, suppressed pass-through glyph, tee + caps). |

| 2026-06-26 | **iSystem — cut-plan efficiency engine extended to BJLS / PU ducting** | "Also do the same for BJLS and PU ducting." Ducts are fabricated in standard **section** lengths joined by flanges, so the same 1D section/offcut optimisation applies. New `ductSectionLengthM` (BJLS galvanised-steel sheet ≈ **1.2 m**, PU pre-insulated panel ≈ **4 m**; null product ⇒ BJLS; `// VERIFY` representative practice) + `stockLengthForEdge` (pipes by service, ducts by `DuctProduct`). `buildPipeChains`/`buildPipeCutPlan` now key on (service, diameter, **stock length**) so a chain breaks at a BJLS↔PU transition and ducts plan against their own section length; `PipeChain` carries `stockLengthM`. The on-canvas joint marks dropped the air exclusion — ducts now show **flange joints** at the section boundary along the merged chain (BJLS dense at 1.2 m, PU at 4 m), and the Review **Pipe cut plan** card labels ducts by Ø + BJLS/PU with their section length. Pure-additive; no sizing/hydraulic change. Gate: engine **721** (+4 duct section/cut tests), app **272**, analyze clean; goldens unchanged (demo has no calibrated ducts), visually verified on a calibrated BJLS supply-duct preview (1.2 m flanges + caps). |

| 2026-06-26 | **iSystem — duct material defaults by install: PU for AC, BJLS for exhaust** | "It should automatically default to PU for AC installs, BJLS only for exhaust fan installs." New `defaultDuctProductForService` (supply `duct` + `returnAir` → **PU** pre-insulated panel; `exhaust` → **BJLS** galvanised steel) + `effectiveDuctProductFor(edge)` = explicit `ductProduct` else the service default, both in `network.dart`. Wired as the SINGLE resolver everywhere a duct's product was previously read from null: `duct_static` roughness (so an AC duct now defaults to the smoother PU friction → lower fan static; exhaust stays galvanised), `pipe_optimizer.stockLengthForEdge` (PU 4 m vs BJLS 1.2 m sections / flange spacing / cut plan), and the on-canvas material tag (`_productTag`). This redefines the duct half of the "null product ⇒ byte-identical" guardrail (CLAUDE.md updated): a null-product AC duct is now PU, not galvanised — deliberate. | 2026-06-26 | **iSystem — per-segment duct sheet-material takeoff (m² + sheets)** | "Show the calculation if we select a segment of duct — how much material is used (m²) and sheets." New pure-engine `ductSheetTakeoff(edge, sizing, lengthM)` → `DuctSheetTakeoff{developedAreaM2, sheetAreaM2, sheets, thicknessMm, product}`: developed sheet area = **perimeter × length** (rect: 2(W+H); round: πD), divided by the standard stock sheet → `sheets = ceil(area ÷ sheet)`. Standard sheet sizes added to `duct_products.dart` (`ductSheetAreaM2`: **BJLS 4×8 ft = 1.22×2.44 m**, **PU panel 1.2×4 m**; `// VERIFY` representative). Surfaced both where a segment is inspected: the **right-click duct menu** (a "Sheet material: X m² (perimeter × length) = N sheets @ Y m²" note, for any sized air segment) and the **inspector Selection editor** (`_edgeEditor`), using the effective (service-defaulted) product. Pure-additive, no sizing change. Gate: engine **726** (+4 takeoff tests, hand-derived), app **273** (+a right-click duct-takeoff widget test), analyze clean; goldens unchanged. |

| 2026-06-26 | **iSystem — ducting accessories takeoff (covering angle, gasket, hangers, bolts)** | "What about calculating the ducting accessories needed such as covering angle etc." New pure-engine `computeDuctAccessories(edge, sizing, lengthM)` → `DuctAccessories`: from the section count (`ceil(L ÷ section)`) + duct perimeter it derives the **covering / flange angle (siku)** length = `2 × sections × perimeter` (each piece framed both ends), the **joint gasket** = `sections × perimeter`, the **hanger supports** = `ceil(L ÷ 2.4 m)`, and the **flange bolt sets** = `frames × max(4, ceil(perimeter ÷ 0.15 m))`. Hanger spacing + bolt pitch are `// VERIFY` representative SMACNA/Indonesian practice (parameters, defaulted). Surfaced on both segment-inspection surfaces alongside the sheet takeoff: the **right-click duct menu** ("Accessories: 25.1 m covering angle · 12.6 m gasket · 5 hangers · 180 bolt sets") and the inspector **Selection editor**. Pure-additive, no sizing change. Gate: engine **728** (+2 accessories tests, hand-derived), app **273** (the duct-menu widget test extended), analyze clean; goldens unchanged. |

| 2026-06-26 | **iSystem — jointing-consumables estimate (PVC cement cans, sealant cartridges, tape rolls)** | "Can it calculate how many bottles of PVC glue, sealant etc for an estimate?" New pure-engine `sizing/consumables.dart`: `joinMethodForService` (PVC **solvent-weld** for drainage/vent/storm, PPR **heat-fusion** for cold/hot water → no cement, **threaded** steel for fire), `fittingSockets` (elbow 2/tee 3/cross 4/reducer 2), VERIFY-tagged coverage figures (`pvcCementMlPerJoint(dn)=max(2,0.06·dn)`, 1 L cement tin, 11 m/300 ml sealant cartridge, 30 joints/tape roll), and `estimateConsumables({solventJointsByDn, ductSealMetres, threadedJoints})` → `ConsumablesEstimate` (cement cans, sealant cartridges, tape rolls, all `ceil`). App `consumablesProvider` gathers joints from `fittingsProvider` (sockets per service join-method) + inline couplings from `pipeChainsProvider` (sections−1) and duct sealant from the per-edge `computeDuctAccessories(...).gasketM`. Surfaced as a **Consumables (estimate)** card in the Review hub (cans/cartridges/rolls + the joint counts + a "verify per datasheet; PPR is heat-fused" caption). Pure-additive, no sizing change. | 2026-06-26 | **iSystem — chain electrical loads onto one breaker (e.g. two adjacent sockets)** | "We should be able to chain loads together so they combine into 1 load in the panel — e.g. two sockets next to each other." New `ElectricalProjectController.mergeCircuit(panelId, sourceId, targetId)`: on the SAME panel the source way's load (watts + motor kW) is folded into the target and the source is removed — one breaker now carries both. The target keeps its identity/cable/phase; refuses same-id, a feeder either side, or unknown ids; field-preserving via `_withProject`. UI: on the single-line canvas a load node is now a `DragTarget<_LoadRef>` as well as a `Draggable` — dropping a load onto **another load** chains them (same panel) or **re-parents** (different panel, via `moveCircuit`); the panel-card re-parent target is unchanged. The sizing engine already exposed per-edge `velocity`, so duct checks need no new maths. **Model:** additive `NetNode.faceWidthMm`/`faceHeightMm` (a manually chosen grille/diffuser face) — constructor + copyWith (`clearFace`) + all four node-rebuild paths in `network_store` + a `setNodeFace` controller method + `.mechx` round-trip (`face_w_mm`/`face_h_mm`, tolerant, both null ⇒ byte-identical). **App:** `airVelocityChecksProvider` (per-element `VelocityCheck` keyed by edge/node id over the live sizing + drawn network) + `airWarningCountProvider`; the node inspector gained a **diffuser face-size picker** (standard faces + Auto) with a live face-velocity verdict, the edge inspector shows an **air-velocity verdict** for air ducts, the edge context menu gained a **duct size picker** (standard Ø ladder → `sizeOverride`, mirroring the pipe NPS picker) so ducts can be hand-sized, and the plan paints an **orange "!" badge** on out-of-band air edges + an orange ring on out-of-band terminals. | 2026-06-27 | **iSystem — fire-protection rigour (sprinkler remote-area hydraulics · fire-pump NFPA 20 rating curve)** | Two additive, byte-identical-by-default engine sub-features layered over the existing density/area + standpipe modules, both pure ORCHESTRATION over `hydraulics.dart`/`pump.dart` (no new physics) and `// VERIFY`-tagged. **(1) Sprinkler remote-area hydraulic calc.** New pure `sizing/fire_sprinkler_hydraulic.dart` — the per-head **discharge law** `Q = K·√P` (`headPressureForFlow`/`headFlowForPressure`, K in L/min/bar^0.5, P in bar), and `remoteAreaHydraulics(design, kFactor, minOperatingPressure, branchLength, branchDiameter, hazenWilliamsC)` → `SprinklerRemoteAreaResult`: the most-remote head's required inlet pressure `P = (Q/K)²` for its density/area share, the **branch-line friction** to that head via the engine's own `headLossHazenWilliams` primitive (cross-checked in test), the branch-base pressure (head P + friction), and a **minimum-operating-pressure verdict** (`'Remote head OK'` / `'Remote head under-pressure'`). Defaults: K = 80 (standard 15 mm pendent), min 0.5 bar, 20 m / 25 mm / C = 120 branch — all general fire-engineering practice (`kDefaultPendentKFactor`/`kDefaultMinHeadPressureBar`/`kSprinklerBranchHazenC`), not an SNI clause. **(2) Fire-pump NFPA 20 rating-curve check.** New pure `sizing/fire_pump_rating.dart` — `checkFirePumpRating(ratedFlow, ratedHead, pumpEfficiency, jockeyFlowFraction, designation, standbyRecommended)` → `FirePumpRatingResult`: the three acceptance points **churn** (0 % flow, head = 140 % rated), **rated** (100 %/100 %), **overload** (150 % flow, head = 65 % rated) each with `shaftPower` from the `shaftPower` primitive; the motor selected (`selectMotor`) on the governing (rated) point with an **'Oversized pump curve'** flag when the standard frame saturates; a **jockey-pump** recommendation (1 % of rated flow @ churn head + 5 m margin); and a **duty/standby** designation (`PumpDesignation`, standby recommended by default). Curve ratios (`kChurnHeadRatio` 1.40, `kOverloadHeadRatio` 0.65, `kOverloadFlowRatio` 1.50, `kJockeyFlowFraction` 0.01) are the NFPA 20 acceptance *limits* (`secondarySource`/`// VERIFY`, not a specific pump's certified curve). Wiring: `lib/store/fire_store.dart` gains `firePerHeadKFactorProvider` (default 80), `sprinklerRemoteAreaProvider`, `firePumpRatingProvider` (off the existing sprinkler/standpipe design providers); the calc report (`CalcReportData.sprinklerRemoteArea`/`firePumpRating`, both nullable ⇒ section unchanged when absent) renders the remote-head P/verdict + the pump curve/jockey/duty lines under **Fire protection**; `project_panel` threads both providers into the report data. No `.mechx` change (providers derive from existing state; no new persisted settings). Gate: engine **843** (+`fire_sprinkler_hydraulic_test` 16, +`fire_pump_rating_test` 14, all hand-derived), app non-golden green (+`fire_store_test` remote-area/rating/K-factor cases), full analyze clean; no golden impact (Fire-section additions only render when the new nullable fields are present, which the app now always supplies but the EN report strings/goldens are unaffected). |

| 2026-06-27 | **iSystem — pump/fan operating point + NPSH (system × equipment curve)** | A new additive, byte-identical-by-default engine sub-feature giving the *true* operating point of a sized pump/fan (where the rising **system-resistance curve** crosses the falling **equipment curve**) plus, for pumps, an NPSH cavitation check — all pure ORCHESTRATION over `hydraulics.dart` (no new physics), `// VERIFY`-tagged. **New `sizing/operating_point.dart`:** `computePumpOperatingPoint(designFlow, designHead, {systemStaticHead, systemResistanceK, suctionStaticHead, …})` → `PumpOperatingPoint` and `computeFanOperatingPoint(designAirflow, designPressure, {systemResistanceK})` → `FanOperatingPoint`. System curve = `H_sys = H_static + k·Q²` (pump) / `Δp_sys = k·Q²` (fan, no static term); equipment curve = a representative falling parabola pinned to the design point with a 125 % shutoff (`kEquipmentShutoffHeadRatio`). The intersection is **closed form** (`Q_op = √((H_shutoff − H_static)/(k + a))`) — no iteration — with a `stable` flag set false on no-real-root (machine starved by excess static head) or a near-tangent/flat crossing. **k is a DESIGN INPUT, never fitted from the network:** when omitted it is back-solved so the system curve passes through the design point (pump default = a representative 50/50 static:friction split). **NPSH (pumps):** `NPSH_available = (P_atm − P_vapour)/(ρg) + h_suction_static − h_suction_friction`, vs a `// VERIFY` `NPSH_required` estimate (`0.15·H_design`, scaled by `(Q_op/Q_design)²`), with `cavitationRisk` flagged CONSERVATIVELY when `NPSH_a < 1.5·NPSH_r` (`kNpshSafetyMargin`). All curve coefficients (shutoff ratio, droop, NPSH_required fraction, safety margin, atmospheric/vapour pressures, suction-friction fraction) are representative `secondarySource`/`// VERIFY` estimates, NOT certified machine data. **Composition:** `PumpDuty`/`FanDuty` each gain an optional `operatingPoint` field; `sizePump`/`sizeFan` gain `withOperatingPoint` + system-curve params and populate it ONLY when requested (absent ⇒ null ⇒ byte-identical). `SizingContext` gains additive `systemHeadStatic`/`systemResistanceK`/`airSystemResistanceK` design-input fields (default null). The calc report (`CalcReportData.pumpOperatingPoint`/`fanOperatingPoint`, falling back to the duty's own field) renders the operating point + stability + (pump) NPSH lines under Water supply / HVAC when present. No `.mechx` change (system-curve coefficients are sizing-context design inputs, not yet persisted — a future DesignSettings wave). Gate: engine **873** (+`operating_point_test` 30, hand-derived closed-form intersections + NPSH arithmetic + composition byte-identity), full analyze clean; no golden impact (operating-point report lines only render when the new nullable fields are present, and the app does not yet supply them, so the EN report/goldens are unaffected). |

Component primitives (`transmissionGainW`/`solarGainW`/`ventilationSensibleW`/`ventilationLatentW`) are public so tests hand-verify each stream. Every coefficient (U-values wall 2.5/roof 1.5/glass 3.0, SHGC 0.65, irradiance 300 W/m², person 75 W sens / 55 W lat, lighting 10 / equip 12 W/m², 0.1 people/m², design ΔT 9 K / Δw 0.010 kg/kg, 1 ACH outdoor air, air ρ 1.2 / cp 1005 / h_fg 2.45 MJ/kg) is a single representative steady-state default, `secondarySource`/`// VERIFY`, surfaced via `detailedCoolingVerifyChecklist` — NOT a CLTD/RTS transient solve, NOT a confirmed SNI clause. The simple module stays the fallback. **(2) Multi-zone aggregation.** `room_air.dart` gains `multiZoneAirSystem(zones, diversityFactor, exhaustStrategy, …)` → `MultiZoneResult`: sums several already-sized `RoomAirResult`s onto one central AHU, applies a **diversity/coincidence factor** k ∈ (0,1] (default 0.9, `kDefaultZoneDiversityFactor`, `// VERIFY`) to the summed supply airflow, re-sizes the central duty via `sizeFan` against the diversified airflow at the **governing** (worst-zone) static pressure, and notes the **make-up/exhaust balance** per `ExhaustStrategy` (returnAll/supplyAll/balanced — supply-all ⇒ no return ⇒ full net-supply imbalance), advisory only. Pure summation + the diversity scale; no air physics added. Per-zone `ZoneContribution` airflow fractions for report transparency. **Persistence:** `DesignSettings` gains `coolingLoadMethod` (`'simple'`/`'detailed'`, default `'simple'`, tolerant), `multiZoneDiversityFactor` (default 0.9, clamped to (0,1] on load), `multiZoneExhaustStrategy` (`'return_all'`/`'supply_all'`/`'balanced'`, default `'return_all'`, tolerant) — all additive, absent ⇒ default ⇒ byte-identical, no version bump. The DrawingOverlay publishes the live cursor into `drawHoverProvider` (read on commit, never watched, so hover churn never rebuilds the bar); the bar computes the target and calls the existing `placeRunPoint` (no signature change), and is mounted only while drawing — idle/goldens byte-identical. Never invents geometry or resizes — safer than AutoCAD bulk edits. **(4) Offset run** (`NetworkController.offsetEdgeParallel` + `ui/canvas/offset_dialog.dart`): right-click a run on a calibrated sheet → "Offset…" → distance (mm) + side → a parallel run at that offset in one undo step (two nodes + one edge of the same service; calibration-agnostic, the dialog converts mm→px). | 2026-06-27 | **iSystem — in-app Claude copilot (command registry · plan→preview→confirm→apply · BYO key)** | Integrated Claude INTO the app so the engineer selects a room/element and asks the AI to design or change it — Claude proposes a plan, the engineer reviews every step, and on confirm each step runs through the existing controllers (one undo apiece; sizing recomputes reactively). Offline-first: no silent edits, no crashes, the rest of the app unaffected without a key. Designed via a 4-layer parallel scoping workflow, then built sequentially. **(1) Command registry** (`lib/ai/commands.dart`): a typed, CLOSED `AiCommand` set — `placeComponent`/`placeTerminal`/`placeFitting`/`placeSegment`, `autoPlaceRoomTerminals`, `suggest` — each a pure JSON-round-trippable value with a plain-language `preview` + the Anthropic tool-use `input_schema`. An unknown `kind` decodes to **null** (hallucination guard), so the AI can never name a method the app doesn't implement. **(2) Injectable client** (`lib/ai/ai_client.dart`): `AiClient` interface + `AnthropicAiClient` (raw HTTP `POST /v1/messages`, tool-use, BYO key, model `claude-sonnet-4-6`, 30 s timeout, system prompt describing the app) + `FakeAiClient` for tests. ALL failures are TYPED (`AiResult` = `AiOk`/`AiDisabled`/`AiError`) — never an exception: empty key ⇒ disabled, network/401/timeout/malformed ⇒ error. The Messages-API response is decoded by mapping each `tool_use` block → `AiCommand`, `text` blocks → rationale. **(3) Copilot store** (`lib/store/ai_copilot_store.dart`): `CopilotController` drives the loop (idle → thinking → proposed → idle/error). `ask()` gathers a COMPACT context snapshot (selection + room ids + network counts — kept small for the token budget), calls the injected client, and stores the proposed plan WITHOUT applying; `applyPlan()` runs each command via the matching `NetworkController` method; `discard()` drops it. `aiClientProvider` is overridable so tests inject `FakeAiClient` with no key/network. **(4) UI** (`lib/ui/ai/copilot_panel.dart`): a right-side `CopilotOverlay` — input, thinking, plan preview (each command's `preview` + a change count), Apply/Discard. Gated on `copilotOpenProvider` ⇒ **`SizedBox.shrink` when closed**, so the goldens are byte-identical. Discoverable via a new **"Ask Claude"** command-palette action (palette closed in goldens ⇒ no shift). **(5) BYO key** persists via additive `DesignSettings.anthropicApiKey`/`aiModel` (tolerant load) wired through autosave build/apply + `aiApiKeyProvider`/`aiModelProvider`; an **API-key card** in Preferences (masked, never displays the key, warns not to share a `.mechx` carrying it). Gate: app **382** (+`ai_copilot_test` 11 offline — command round-trip, hallucination guard, plan-not-applied-until-confirm + undo, discard, empty-plan, disabled-without-key, persistence; +`copilot_panel_test` 2 — golden-safe-when-closed, opens + disabled-without-key), full analyze clean, goldens unchanged (overlay closed by default; palette action + Preferences card not in the screenshot set); engine untouched. The live Anthropic call needs the engineer's key to exercise; the registry + plan loop are fully covered offline via `FakeAiClient`. Future: connect-two-nodes / draw-run-between-ids command, an i18n pass for the panel/Preferences strings, OS secure-storage for the key. |

New `lib/ai/openai_client.dart` (`OpenAiAiClient` → `POST https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions`, `Authorization: Bearer`; the command registry mapped to OpenAI function-tools by a pure `openAiToolsFromRegistry()`; `tool_calls[].function.arguments` JSON decoded by a pure `parseOpenAiResponse` → `AiCommand`; `message.content` → rationale; same TYPED `AiResult` ok/disabled/error — empty key⇒disabled, 401/403/timeout/offline/malformed⇒error, never throws; built on its own worktree by a background agent, no edits to `ai_client.dart`/`commands.dart`). `ai_client.dart` gains `AiProviderKind`/`kDefault{Anthropic,OpenAi}Model`/`defaultModelForProvider`/`aiProviderFromName`; `aiProviderProvider` (`app_state.dart`) drives `aiClientProvider`'s switch (Anthropic⇒`AnthropicAiClient`, OpenAI⇒`OpenAiAiClient`); the store resolves a **family-correct model** in `ask()` (`_effectiveModel` — a `claude-*` string is never sent to OpenAI nor a `gpt-*` to Anthropic, even from a hand-edited file). Persists additively via `DesignSettings.aiProvider` (tolerant load, unknown→anthropic) through autosave build/apply; a **Provider** Anthropic/OpenAI toggle in the Preferences AI card (the active one shown as the filled/disabled button) re-seeds the model field to that provider's default on switch. **Subscription/OAuth sign-in is deliberately NOT built**: a live browser PKCE flow needs an Anthropic-registered OAuth client ID that third-party apps can't obtain today, and consumer ChatGPT login ≠ API access — so rather than ship dead OAuth scaffolding (against the declutter direction) the honest shippable path stays **BYO-key for either provider**. **(B) Riser-view discoverability (declutter-consistent).** After renaming Schematic→Riser, two flagged confusions got ON-DEMAND clarity (behind the existing `?` canvas/help guides — hidden by default, so goldens stay byte-identical) instead of persistent prose: the Layout canvas mechanical guide gains a line that risers drawn on the plan stack vertically in the Riser view (closing the "two riser entry points" gap), and the Riser-view Edit help legend now LEADS with an Auto-vs-Edit mode explainer (`StringKey.schematicHelpModes`, EN+ID). | 2026-06-28 | **iSystem — audit-fix wave (robustness + safety + validation)** | A read-only audit (`AUDIT-REPORT.md`, file:line + fix per finding) drove a batch of correctness/safety fixes, all additive and byte-identical-by-default unless a finding required correcting a wrong number. **Cluster A (engine sizing).** Finding #1 (CRITICAL — an over-target air duct THREW `ArgumentError`, aborting the whole solve): `sizing/duct_sizing.dart` `DuctSizingResult`/`RectangularDuctResult` gained `final bool overCapacity` (default false); `sizeByVelocity`/`sizeByEqualFriction`/`sizeRectangularByVelocity` now CLAMP to the largest standard size + flag `overCapacity` (with the actual over-target friction) instead of `orElse: throw`. `network_sizing.dart` `EdgeSizing` gained `overCapacity` threaded through every air sub-case of `sizeEdge`; app-side new `airOverCapacityProvider` (`store/air_warnings_store.dart`) fans an over-capacity air edge into `designIssuesProvider` as a per-edge `IssueSeverity.warning` "Duct over capacity" with `IssueLocation(sheetId, edgeId)`. Finding #10 (MEDIUM — rectangular ducts ignored equal-friction): new `sizeRectangularByEqualFriction` (pure orchestration over `rectangularEquivalentDiameter`→`ductFrictionPaPerMetre`, clamp+flag), honoured in `network_sizing` + `room_air` rectangular branches when `ctx.ductMethod == equalFriction` (velocity path untouched). Finding #11 (MEDIUM — looped water over-sized the ring source): the looped-component branch now accumulates RAW per-node UBAP, curves the SUMMED units ONCE via `probableFlowForFixtureUnits`, then splits the diversified total by each node's UBAP share before Hardy-Cross (mirrors the tree path; f(50+50)=0.00303 vs the old f(50)+f(50)=0.00367). Polish: corrected the `drainage_sizing` `sizeForFlow` docstring to the true partial-full method. **Cluster B (electrical safety).** Finding #2 (CRITICAL — cable In>Iz was silent): `results.dart` `CableResult` gained `final bool ampacityReached` (default true; vd-only fallback → true, ampacity-impossible → false); `compute.dart` emits a `WarningSeverity.error` `cable-ampacity-inadequate` (IEC 60364-4-43 / PUIL 2.2.8.3, names Iz vs In). Finding #3 (HIGH — TT feeders had no earth-fault protection): `earthing.dart` `circuitRcd` adds a TT-FEEDER branch (required 300 mA S-type time-delayed RCD, selective above finals, reason `// VERIFY`); `fault.dart` emits a `tt-no-earth-fault-protection` warning for a TT way covered by neither ADS nor an RCD (TN unchanged, TT finals now carry an RCD so they no longer warn). Finding (MEDIUM — motor FLC understated): `compute.dart` divides hand-entered motor shaft kW by a file-level `_assumedMotorEfficiency = 0.88` (`// VERIFY` `secondarySource`, deliberately NOT in a standards profile) before deriving Ib; the A5 `flaOverrideA` feed path is untouched. Re-derived the affected `electrical_compute_test` motor circuit (Ib 12.7→14.5 A) + its downstream phase-balance / section-current / imbalance to the new first-principles values (breaker/cable/incomer/busbar all stayed on the same ladder rung). **Cross-cutting (uncovered by other clusters): uncalibrated-zero-length + unfed-network validation, empty-electrical fallback, export feedback + gate, and assorted UI state fixes; goldens regenerated where the intended states changed.** Gate: engine **981** (Cluster A → 974, Cluster B → 981; new hand-derived cases — duct velocity/equal-friction clamp+`overCapacity` default-false, rectangular equal-friction smallest-qualifying + clamp, looped-water UBAP-diversified-once + continuity, `cable-ampacity-inadequate`, TT-feeder RCD + `tt-no-earth-fault-protection`, motor-efficiency Ib uplift), app electrical/issues/air-warnings/project-document suites green, full analyze clean from repo root. The `overCapacity` flag is a robustness marker (not a standards value); the new motor-efficiency + TT-RCD-S-type assumptions are `// VERIFY`-tagged and surfaced. ## 16. Testing strategy

- **Engine:** `package:test`, run with `dart test` in `packages/mechx_engine`.
  Seed suites hand-compute expected values from first principles and are the
  correctness anchor. Target **>90% coverage** (`dart test --coverage`).
- **UI:** `flutter_test` widget/integration tests; golden tests for the design
  system once it exists (P0).
- **Continuous green:** no phase advances on red.
